### Queue-space wait and GPU interrupt gap (2026-10-19)

Static code review clarifies that event `0x0021DCF4` is used by generated guest helper `sub_00211E10` as a queue-space wake event. The helper compares the queue producer and completion values; when the outstanding distance reaches `0x2000`, it calls `KeWaitForSingleObject` on the event, then rechecks the counters after waking. The targeted trace's single wait-entry and absent return/set records match that path. The watchdog later found USER `PUT=GET=0x0006CBD0`, so the pushbuffer had drained by the sample, while the guest had not returned from the wait.

The current semaphore-release handler stores the guest completion value, but contains no interrupt delivery. The current runtime also explicitly leaves NV2A interrupt status registers at zero and its kernel bridge comment says no GPU interrupt is delivered. This is the leading explanation for why the guest can remain asleep after completion; it is still a hypothesis until the title's registered GPU ISR/DPC and the release-to-notification sequence are traced. It is separate from USB: USB enumeration did not publish a periodic endpoint, while this queue-space helper concerns the GPU command ring.

Next: identify the exact interrupt routine registered by the guest D3D runtime, determine which NV2A status/enable bits it expects for completion, then implement and verify hardware-equivalent notification through the existing interrupt path. Do not directly set the guest event or hardcode the Steel Battalion event address. No runtime source was changed or rebuilt for this static review. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged.

### Queue-event and vector-3 DPC distinction (2026-10-19)

A cross-check against the generated guest ISR/DPC corrects the scope of the previous interrupt hypothesis. The vector-3 DPC calls `sub_00216BE0`, which invokes the guest event-set import on `context + 0x194`. With the connected vector-3 context `0x0021DB50`, that is event `0x0021DCE4`, the neighboring notification event. The queue-space helper instead waits on `0x0021DCF4` (`0x0021B790 + 0x2564`). These are separate event objects with separate uses. Therefore, the absent GPU-interrupt delivery can explain a missed signal to `0x0021DCE4`, but it does not yet explain the `0x0021DCF4` wait recorded at the latest watchdog. The previous entry's GPU-interrupt diagnosis remains unproven for the queue-space stall.

Next inspect the owner/producer path for `0x0021DCF4` directly, including queue completion callbacks and any alternate event-set sites; correlate it with the semaphore completion store before implementing interrupt delivery. Do not signal either event by address. This is static generated-code/source review only; no source behavior or build changed. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged.

### Candidate producer found in generated NV2A method handling (2026-10-19)

Static review of the generated code found a possible direct producer for the previously blocked queue-space event. The event-setting branch at guest `0x0021701E` in `sub_00216EA0` calls the imported `KeSetEvent` on its method context plus `0x1A4`. That handler is reached through `sub_00217130`, which processes the NV2A context during the guest vector-3 ISR/DPC path. The known vector-3 context base is `0x0021DB50`; adding `0x1A4` yields `0x0021DCF4`, exactly the event object awaited by `sub_00211E10` when the queue fence is full.

This is a stronger candidate than the previously considered DPC signal at `context + 0x194` (event `0x0021DCE4`), and it may explain how the queue wait is intended to resume. Static arithmetic and call-path inspection do not prove that this branch ran, that the actual runtime context was `0x0021DB50` at this call, or that the setter used the expected event handle in the captured stall. Do not alter event behavior based on this inference.

Next: correlate `sub_0021701E` executions and its context pointer with the exact title wait and queue completion during a bounded trace. Then investigate why it is absent or insufficient in the stalled run. No source behavior or build changed; no new runtime test was run. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged. ### DCE4 wait can remain pending (2026-10-19 correction)

A corrected bridge trace captured guest return addresses for five-argument `KeWaitForSingleObject` calls. In a 90-second run, DCE4 (`0x0021DCE4`) had 141 wait entries and 140 successful returns; its final wait remained pending at watchdog expiry. The guest `SignalState` was zero at that wait, and no later DCE4 set was recorded before capture. NV2A USER `PUT=GET=0x00032580`, so this pending wait was not the pushbuffer-full DCF4 case. This corrects the prior conclusion that DCE4 was simply cycling successfully: it returned repeatedly in earlier samples, but can also be the terminal startup wait in another run.

The wait return site was `0x0002DB56`. Generated code maps that to `sub_0002DB50`, which waits through `sub_0020EC60` and then consumes a 16-bit value from a shared ring buffer. Its caller was `0x000DFE7C` in `sub_000DFBC0`, after a sequence of generated graphics/command helper calls. This places the current capture's stall in a ring-buffer consumer path; the exact event producer and why it stopped signaling remain unproven. The separate DCF4 wait and candidate setter at `0x0021701E` were not reached in this run.

Return-address trace caveat: `g_esp+20` produced the verified return site for the five-argument wait bridge. The earlier suggestion to use `g_esp-4` was incorrect. For the three-argument `KeSetEvent` bridge, `g_esp+12` yielded `0xFD000000`, so that set-call return field is invalid; inspect the thunk stack layout before using or reporting KeSetEvent caller addresses.

### NV2A nonzero NOP notification handling (2026-10-19)

A bounded 120-second profile recorded 263 nonzero payloads among the first 275 `NV097_NO_OPERATION` methods. The existing pushbuffer executor ignored this method, and the PGRAPH register handler treated interrupt status and enable registers as ordinary storage. The guest's ISR reads the PGRAPH trapped method and notification source; xemu's reference implementation models a nonzero NOP as a PGRAPH error/notification interrupt, records the trapped method/data, and waits for the guest to acknowledge it by writing the interrupt status register ([xemu PGRAPH implementation](https://github.com/xemu-project/xemu/blob/master/hw/xbox/nv2a/nv2a_pgraph.c#L2783-L2875), [xemu NOP notification method](https://github.com/xemu-project/xemu/blob/master/hw/xbox/nv2a/nv2a_pgraph.c#L3268-L3326)).

Implemented that device behavior in the local runtime and saved it as the isolated dependency patch `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-nv2a-nop-notification.patch`. The Release build succeeded. In a bounded profiled launch, NV2A traces showed the PGRAPH interrupt source asserted and the guest interrupt claimed it; the title art and “PRESS MAIN WEAPON BUTTON” prompt were visible. This verifies the notification/acknowledgement path can run through startup, not that the game is playable.

The same run ended at the 120-second watchdog in the separate queue-space wait on event `0x0021DCF4`, with guest `PUT=0x00080F6C` and `GET=0x0007C52C`. Audio output, a confirmed button response, gameplay, and campaign progression remain unverified. No progress bars advance; Milestone 3 remains active. No game image or generated game code was added to Git.

Next: trace the queue-space producer and the executor's pending segment at the watchdog, including the completion semaphore and the event's set path, then repeat the title-start input with guest-visible controller state.

### DCF4 wait correlated with an in-flight pushbuffer segment (2026-10-19)

A low-overhead 120-second watchdog capture recorded the guest waiting on `0x0021DCF4` while the pushbuffer executor was still scanning a submitted segment. The queue producer was `0x651`, completion was `0x643` (14 entries apart; the helper waits when space falls below its 8-entry threshold), and the DCF4 signal state was zero. NV2A USER `PUT=0x000335D8`, `GET=0x0002A294`. The executor's last completed segment was `0x00025854→0x0002A294`; it was still processing `0x0002A294→0x0002ECEC`. Its last reported cursor was guest `0x8002CCEC`, method `0x17FC` (`SET_BEGIN_END`), count 1, parameter 0. This is consistent with ordinary queue back-pressure while the executor is behind the producer. The snapshot does not prove that method 0x17FC itself is the cause or that the executor was permanently stuck there; it only identifies the method/cursor at watchdog time.

Restored both temporary source traces and rebuilt Release x64 successfully. Verified `xbox_memory_layout.c` returned to baseline SHA-256 `DDBB586B1DBE8B976B23A1662884D1ADF43B89C745684DBF995942E6F57987C3`; verified the rebuilt executable contains no `[PB-STATE]` or `[PB-GUEST]` trace strings and no runtime process remains. The screenshots/logs are ignored locally under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-dcf4-executor-progress-trace-120s-20261019b/`. This does not verify title input, audio output, or gameplay. Progress bars remain unchanged; Milestone 3 remains active.

2026-10-19 XBE thunk mapping for the ring callback: Parsed the user's local XBE kernel thunk table at VA `0x0025D0E0` directly from its section mapping. 2026-10-19 XBE ring producer correlation: Static inspection found that `sub_00216BE0` increments `MEM32(esi + 0x1C0)` then calls the XBE thunk at `0x0025D1E8` with event `esi + 0x194`. The local XBE thunk table maps slot 66 (`0x0025D1E8`) to ordinal 145, `KeSetEvent`; slot 67 (`0x0025D1EC`) is ordinal 159, `KeWaitForSingleObject`. Earlier DCE4 tracing records 7,190 `KeSetEvent` calls from guest return site `0x00216CAE`. For the traced DCE4 event at `0x0021DCE4`, the callback's `esi` base is therefore `0x0021DB50`, making its incremented field `0x0021DD10`. This matches the ring producer field exposed through pointer `0x0021B790 + 0x2580`. This is strong evidence that the device callback publishes the same counter consumed by `sub_0002DB50`, and that DCE4 is its associated notification event. It does not show whether the 25-item consumer request is expected to be fulfilled at that startup stage, nor whether callback scheduling stops or stalls in the intermittent Dolby capture. No runtime source change or test; generated analysis and XBE remain local. M3/progress bars unchanged. Next compare callback invocations, producer count, and the 25-item predicate across short samples in a title-reaching configuration and a Dolby-stalled configuration; do not synthesize event signals.

Several are guarded by device-state bits, and `sub_00216CF0` invokes it after a separate helper call. This establishes multiple state-dependent callers but does not identify a single scheduler or prove which path supplies the updates observed during the successful title capture. ### 2026-10-19 crash-site static follow-up

Mapped the keyboard-run crash's guest return address `0x00221331` against the local XBE translation and instruction listing. It returns into `sub_002212F4`, which calls `sub_0021E780`; the latter is a reference-count release helper that reads `[object+4]`, decrements it, and calls the object's first vtable method if the count reaches zero. At failure, the supplied object pointer was `0xFE0001D4`, so the faulting read at `0xFE0001D8` is outside the mapped XBE guest image. The guest stack also includes return address `0x002241EA` in `sub_00224115`, which the translation labels `game_audio` and whose nearby logic traverses audio-related lists and invokes callbacks.

This narrows the observed failure to an invalid guest object pointer during audio/resource cleanup. The producer of that pointer is not known; the stack classification does not prove a DirectSound API mismatch, and the single run does not show that enabling `RECOMP_KEYBOARD` caused it. No fix is justified yet. Next add a capped diagnostic at the release helper/caller to capture the object and list-entry values before the access, then compare matched launches with and without keyboard fallback. Preserve the faulting behavior during diagnosis rather than silently skipping the release. Progress bars and Milestone 3 remain unchanged.

### 2026-10-19 OHCI enabled and title-input report

### 2026-10-19 pushbuffer-enabled XID startup

The control-only USB stalls were caused by the diagnostic launch omitting `RECOMP_PB_EXEC=1`. Static inspection and the watchdog's guest registers identify the no-executor spin at `sub_00211E10`'s fence loop: it waits until the completion word at `0x80003000` reaches the caller's target. In the non-executing run, the producer advanced to 11 while the completion word remained 3, so the report endpoint was never initialized. This explains those particular setup-only runs; it is not a controller-protocol failure.

Repeated the bounded run with `RECOMP_PB_EXEC=1`. The guest fence completed (the completion word advanced from 3 to 7), then USB enumeration issued the 26-byte `GET_REPORT` and began periodic XID polling. A 60-second capture served 2,247 periodic reports; a 180-second capture served 10,322. In the longer run, appending a live `a` press changed input-report byte 2 bit 0 to `1`, then returned it to `0`, matching the documented Main Weapon field. This verifies the virtual profile's transport and report mapping under this startup configuration, not game-side input acceptance or a physical controller.

The 180-second run's final framebuffer capture is black; this run does not verify the title screen or a visible response to the input. Keep the earlier title-screen capture as a separate one-run result. The virtual XID progress bar remains 1/3: report delivery is verified; guest state and accepted title action remain unverified. Original Steel Battalion hardware remains 0/3.

### 2026-10-19 byte-verified startup texture cache

Profiled the window-enabled logo draw by stage. The Vulkan submission/setup, queue wait, and readback path took about 8.0–10.2 ms; the first complete draw took 60.6–61.2 ms. The CPU still converted the same 512x512 DXT1 splash texture for every fade update. Added a one-texture decoded-RGBA cache to the existing exact logo path. It is restricted to the observed 512x512 DXT1 format and only reuses decoded pixels after comparing the full 128 KiB guest texture image against a saved copy; a changed offset or changed source bytes triggers a fresh decode. This does not assume guest texture memory is immutable.

A 30-second window-enabled run logged one cache refresh and four subsequent byte-verified hits. Later logo draws measured 34.3–35.7 ms, versus 61.2 ms for the first decode in the same run (about 42% lower). The output uses the same cached conversion of unchanged source bytes; the Vulkan submission path itself is unchanged. The 25-second guest watchdog still did not reach the Steel Battalion title screen, and this startup sample is not a gameplay or steady-state frame-rate benchmark. No progress bars advance.

The reusable change is isolated in `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-vulkan-logo-texture-cache.patch`. Verified that the patch applies to the saved post-Vulkan-logo baseline and reproduces the local modified source hash. Removed temporary timing code from the framebuffer presenter, restored the kernel source except for the cache change, rebuilt the Release x64 target successfully, verified no temporary gate/timing markers remain in the executable, and confirmed no game process remains. User's modified XID profile patch is untouched.

Next profile the remaining roughly 27 ms between the total draw and measured Vulkan stage, including time waiting for the presenter queue lock. Then measure and improve the per-draw surface readback/serialization without changing guest timing or raster semantics. ### 2026-10-20 windowed startup presentation handoff

Rebuilt the current `build-xpp-seeded` Release x64 target; an initial diagnostic had accidentally used the older October 17 `build/Release` binary and its black-frame result was discarded. A matched windowed run with the current executable reached 95 flips and captured the Nude Maker startup logo in the actual presenter output at 640x480. A traced run showed the completed guest framebuffer alternates between `0x80084000` and `0x801B0000`; logo-stage frames contained about 11,000–13,000 nonblack pixels at the present callback, and those pixels were copied into the window thread's upload buffer. The callback can advance twice before a UI poll, returning to the same alternating buffer index. The old index-only change test could then mistake a new frame for the already shown one.

Added an isolated follow-up patch, `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-vulkan-frame-generation.patch`, based on the existing Vulkan framebuffer presenter patch. It serializes the guest-to-host frame copy with an SRW lock and tracks an interlocked generation counter, so a reused buffer index still signals a fresh frame. The patch passes `git apply --check` against the saved post-presenter baseline. The current Release x64 target rebuilt successfully with the trace-only probes removed. The matched 36-second watchdog run captured the Nude Maker logo and 95 flips; a separate later capture ended on a black frame after the startup stage. That later frame does not establish title-screen boot or a stable running scene.

This is a verified startup-logo presentation result only. The game did not reach its title prompt in the matched run. Audio output, accepted guest input, gameplay, saves, and campaign progress remain unverified. No performance bar or milestone bar advances; Milestone 3 remains active. User's modified Steel Battalion XID profile patch was not touched. Logs and captures remain local/ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-*-20261020/`.

Reviewed additional public GitHub recompilation resources in response to the title-stage stall. The pinned XboxRecomp v0.13.1 workflow already provides the XBE parsing, function-splitting/recompilation pipeline, Ghidra naming workflow, and native Windows runtime used here. A separate [MIT-licensed Xbox XBE Recomp](https://github.com/TGP482/XboxXBERecomp) translates XBE code ahead of time and has its own kernel/XDK and D3D12 runtime; its README uses a distinct Clang/Ninja and generated-project layout. This is a plausible independent comparison project, but not a compatible drop-in fix for the observed guest wait/render progression, so the active build and renderer architecture remain unchanged. Its license is permissive, but no source or dependency was copied. The installed Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools built the current Release target successfully after restoring the temporary DCF4 filter; no new toolchain installation was needed. A separate public [XboxRecomp getting-started guide](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/docs/GETTING_STARTED.md) recommends using xemu's GDB stub as an optional behavioral reference, which is a suitable next comparison for title/input behavior without substituting emulation for the delivered static-recompiled executable.

Also reviewed the community [Xemu-SB-VK fork](https://github.com/quizerno/Xemu-SB-VK) and [Steel Battalion controller guide](https://github.com/quizerno/Steel-Battalion-Entry-Guide/blob/main/3-Controller%20Inputs.md). The fork is an emulator project with USB passthrough and keyboard rebinding; its own notes list direct joystick binding, pedal/tuner rebinding, and controller-light behavior as unfinished or incomplete. These feature priorities reinforce the need to keep analog controls, toggles, gear/tuner behavior, and indicator outputs distinct in our input compatibility work, but the emulator fork is not a static-recompilation runtime and no code was copied. The community guide documents many discrete controls alongside analog axes and pedals. The public [xemu Steel Battalion compatibility report](https://xemu.app/titles/43430002/) says an older test reached the main menu but registered no input; that is a historical emulator result, not a diagnosis of this native runtime. This machine has no xemu installation or configured Xbox firmware, so the optional GDB comparison cannot run yet; the next local test will continue with a low-overhead trace of the guest controller-state routine and its output buffer.

The traced function was called 141 times from guest return site `0x00049EAA`, with destination `0x004AF670`.

The queried buffer is the game's ordinary XInput result, not the custom Steel Battalion USB report. Its packet counter advanced while its regular button/trigger fields stayed neutral and the centered stick fields remained `0x00800080`. That is useful separation: the standard XInput path did not mirror the synthetic Steel Battalion USB report, but it says nothing yet about the custom USB callback's state. The capture therefore does not establish guest acceptance or rejection of the Main Weapon control. The frame remained very dark and the renderer reported roughly 0.12–0.27 FPS during the captured later stage. At the 140-second watchdog the guest was in the DCF4 queue fence (`ESI=0x0021DCF4`); NV2A USER DMA showed `PUT=0x72650`, `GET=0x6A87C`. This snapshot identifies a queue backlog but not why it formed.

### 2026-10-20 Steel Battalion report layout correction

The previous OHCI DMA trace showed that the initial 26-byte report reached mapped guest memory at the reported CBP, but the analog values were displaced by one byte. Compared the report layout with an Xbox-side Steel Battalion XID test that declares the packed fields as bytes 0-1 header, bytes 2-7 digital controls, bytes 8-9 aiming X, 10-11 aiming Y, 12-13 rotation, 14-17 sight axes, 18-23 pedals, byte 24 tuner, and byte 25 gear ([public test gist](https://gist.github.com/Ryzee119/5b74e94eb765bcc54e68afde9ddc7623)). The existing profile wrote each analog field one byte late. Corrected those four axis field offsets in the isolated patch `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-steel-battalion-xid-report-offsets.patch`; the user's existing XID profile patch was not modified.

The patch passed `git apply --check` against the local profile source and passed reverse-application validation after applying. Visual Studio 2022 Release x64 rebuilt successfully. A 76-second bounded run emitted the expected 26-byte report: the synthetic A step set byte 2 bit 0, and both neutral aiming axes were centered as `00 80` at bytes 8-9 and 10-11. OHCI serviced 7,215 reports in the captured stats. This verifies report layout and transport only; the game did not render past its first flip in this short run, and guest-side acceptance, title progression, audio, gameplay, saves, and campaign remain unverified. The watchdog reported no guest return at 70 seconds; the harness process was then stopped. Logs are ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-xid-layout-fix-20261020/`.

The earlier DMA-trace run itself stopped making useful progress and hit its 135-second watchdog after 329 periodic transfers. It logged a mapped neutral report at CBP `0x010816C3`; it does not establish the later input transition. Temporary OHCI tracing was restored byte-for-byte to SHA-256 `52C4E93766A84962803BD4E963FD24D79041C94F483707F8CC827172FDA257A5`, and the target was rebuilt. Milestone 3 and all progress bars remain unchanged. Next, trace the game's USB completion-state object with the corrected payload layout, while investigating the title's one-flip/queue progression separately.

This confirms the OHCI DMA completion reaches the game's USB/XID completion routine. It does not show the action being consumed as game input or accepted by the title prompt.

### 2026-10-20 guest XID payload-state trace

Followed the completion path downstream from `sub_0025BFA3` through its dynamically selected callback `sub_00259A31` to target `0x0025D059`. That routine reads the report length, copies the 24-byte body from context offset `+0x34` to the guest state at `+0x14` (removing the two-byte XID header), then checks the first three 16-bit button words. Its nonzero path tail-calls `sub_00193C94`; this call relationship is established from static control flow, not a separately captured runtime entry.

A temporary trace sampled the post-copy guest buffer at `0x010816A5` during the scripted A press/release. The first button word changed from `0x0000` to `0x0001` while held and returned to `0x0000` after release. The aiming axes were centered (`00 80`). XboxDevWiki's Steel Battalion controller-to-Xbox table maps report byte 2 bit 0 to `RightJoyMainWeapon`, confirming the test bit's documented meaning ([Xbox Input Devices](https://xboxdevwiki.net/Xbox_Input_Devices)). This verifies report delivery into the game's guest-side input state. It does not verify that `sub_00193C94` executes dynamically, that the title prompt accepts the action, or that physical hardware is supported.

The bounded 45-second sample still rendered only one flip at 0.00 FPS; the watchdog found no guest return with NV2A USER `PUT=GET=0x495C`. It did not reproduce the title prompt and is not campaign or gameplay evidence. After collecting it, all temporary traces were restored. No game process remains; captures and logs are ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-xid-dispatch-trace-20261020/`.

The virtual XID path is only partially verified: report mapping and packet changes are observed, and one scripted sample changed the guest button word; a title-synchronized A press changed the USB report but left the callback source and copied guest button words at zero, with the prompt unchanged. Treat guest input delivery as inconsistent and unverified until the USB transfer buffer and callback are correlated in the same title run. ### 2026-10-20 kernel callback stack/register preservation

Compared the pinned XboxRecomp v0.13.1 runtime with the upstream callback fix ([commit 117a649](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/commit/117a6498047c6ab93a48073875a071bff084c9a7)). The local `deliver_one_apc` called the guest completion routine and then advanced guest ESP by a fixed 12 bytes, even though the callback's stdcall return had already consumed its return address and arguments. Reproduced this with upstream's focused inline I/O APC test: before the fix, `NtReadFile` returned at ESP `0x00020030` instead of expected `0x00020024` (a 12-byte overrun). The same upstream fix also preserves the caller's register globals around APC, DPC, `NtUserIoApcDispatcher`, and `KeSynchronizeExecution` callbacks, which run inline on the caller's recompiled thread.

Ported the focused change and adapted the upstream `kernel_io_apc` test for the v0.13.1 API (which does not yet expose deferred APC support). The test intentionally clobbers the callback register globals and verifies both caller-register preservation and exact post-`NtReadFile` ESP. It passed all checks after the fix. The full Steel Battalion Release x64 executable rebuilt successfully with the patched kernel bridge. The isolated patch is `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-kernel-callback-register-preservation.patch`; it applies cleanly in reverse to the modified local dependency, and the test source is included in the patch. XboxRecomp's MIT license was reviewed; no license conflict was found.

A matched windowed startup using the same service/profile flags as the preceding no-fix retry now progressed farther: 321 flips, loaded `advdat00.adv`, and visibly reached the Steel Battalion title prompt. The preceding no-fix windowed run reached 172 flips and did not load that title asset. This is evidence that the callback correction may fix a startup progression fault, not proof of causality or reliable boot; a repeat is still needed. After an `a` was appended to the live input file while the prompt was visible, the prompt remained, and this run did not enable report tracing, so XID delivery or game-side input acceptance is not verified. At the 180-second watchdog the guest was waiting on title event `0x0021DCF4` (`ESI=0x0021DCF4`); NV2A USER PUT=GET=`0x50E8`. The display stopped refreshing at 321 flips (0.00 FPS). Logs and captures are ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-xid-apc-fix-windowed-20261020/`.

The dependency patch is backed by a failing-before/passing-after regression and a successful full game build. No game process remains; the user's controller-profile patch is untouched and unstaged. Progress bars remain unchanged, Milestone 3 stays active. Next repeat the patched windowed boot, add a targeted XID report/state trace after the visible prompt, then investigate why the title event wait and frame updates stop. Audio output, playable input, gameplay, saving, and campaign progression remain unverified.

### 2026-10-20 upstream scheduler research and bounded guest-lock probe

Fetched upstream XboxRecomp tags through v0.15.0 and reviewed its MIT-licensed scheduler and translator changes. The most relevant upstream work is the fair one-guest-CPU scheduler with a 4 ms quantum, a translator `--back-edge-yield` option that checks at guest loop back-edges, and a DISPATCH/interrupt gate. These additions are documented in [upstream commit c7a1817](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/commit/c7a1817) and [scheduler commit 5bb9e04](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/commit/5bb9e04). The current local `RECOMP_GUEST_LOCK` is only a critical section and has no back-edge preemption. The upstream generator patch does not apply cleanly to the locally extended translator, so no bulk upgrade or upstream source change was applied.

The restored ordinary Release target rebuilt through the Visual Studio 2022 bundled CMake successfully. A bounded run with the existing lock enabled rendered only one flip, stayed CPU-busy, never loaded `advdat00.adv`, and produced no watchdog report before the exact diagnostic process was stopped. This is consistent with the upstream warning that a lock without guest loop preemption can deadlock; it does not diagnose the normal unlocked title stall. ### 2026-10-20 guest input state narrowed to title-side polling

Static analysis of `sub_0025D059` shows it scans the copied report words and branches to `sub_00193C94` when a nonzero input is present. `sub_00193C94` calls imported kernel ordinal 149 (`KeSetTimer`) for guest timer `0x006ED4E0` with DPC `0x006ED4C0`; its DPC is initialized to `sub_00193CAF`, which checks guest state and can call ordinal 360 (`HalInitiateShutdown`). This callback path is therefore a device/report event and deferred timer path, not direct evidence of title prompt action dispatch.

### 2026-10-20 Steel Battalion XID protocol cross-check

Compared the current virtual device profile with public XID references. The profile advertises Steel Battalion type/subtype `0x80/0x01` with 26-byte input and 22-byte output reports, matching the [Xbox Input Devices reference](https://xboxdevwiki.net/Xbox_Input_Devices). Because the public offsets conflict, no analog mapping change was retained; analog input remains unverified pending a stronger reference or a hardware capture.

The protocol reference also documents a 22-byte Xbox-to-controller report. The current OHCI runtime accepts and discards endpoint OUT data, so controller indicator/output behavior is not implemented. The opt-in virtual XID device remains a test profile, not physical-controller support. No code or copyrighted game data was copied from the references. A normal Release rebuild completed after reverting the unverified analog-offset experiment; no game process remains. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged.

### 2026-10-20 reproducible XBE seed regeneration

The tracked `analysis/steel-battalion-xbe-function-seeds.json` already contained `0x0025A3D7`, but did not mark it as a runtime-observed address. Added the observation and its source note so XboxRecomp's seed loader can trust the measured indirect-call entry even when its previous linear sweep treated it as an interior boundary. Re-ran `tools.disasm` against the supplied XBE with `--seed-functions`; it recovered exactly one function at `0x0025A3D7–0x0025A52B` (340 bytes, 91 instructions). `tools.func_id` and `tools.abi_analysis` completed, then `tools.recomp --all --split 1000` translated 9,379 functions with zero translation failures. The new dispatch contains `0x0025A3D7`, and the generated unresolved stubs do not.

Configured the local Release build to use that fresh ignored output and successfully rebuilt the Windows target. In a matched 24-second USB startup run, OHCI became operational after about 5.1 seconds and claimed periodic interrupts; the run had no unresolved-call message for `0x0025A3D7`. Logs and all generated code remain local/ignored under `iso/local-analysis/xboxrecomp-dpc-seeded/`. ## 2026-10-20 XID dispatch and contiguous-page collision follow-up
- Added observed XBE callback seed `0x0025D059` to `analysis/steel-battalion-xbe-function-seeds.json`; the existing `0x0025D090` seed was merged into the runtime generation set. v6 disassembly recognized both boundaries, and XboxRecomp generated 9,403 functions with zero translation failures. Ported its opt-in behavior locally and recorded it in `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-steel-battalion-contig-avoid-image.patch`. The diagnostic log confirmed a contiguous allocation overlapping XBE pages `0x00012000..0x00904E48` was moved beyond the image when `RECOMP_CONTIG_AVOID_IMAGE=1`.
- In the patched USB run, OHCI reported up to 2,743 periodic transfers and 2,609 reports, and the renderer reached 171 flips / roughly 14 FPS before presentation stopped. A follow-up also enabled the available upstream guest-CPU lock (4 ms quantum); it reached 165 flips / roughly 14.6 FPS before stopping. Both captures remained black after the opening logos and did not reach the title prompt. No title input was sent. Neither run verifies guest acceptance, audible sound, or gameplay.
- The first v6 run's per-call trace output overwhelmed the diagnostic; later runs used counting-only tracing. Each stalled diagnostic process was stopped, and no game process remains.
- This narrows the startup problem: the callback dispatch and an XPP/OHCI memory collision are addressed, but a later black-frame/presentation stall remains. Do not advance Milestone 3 or project progress bars. Next compare the stalled guest wait/stream path against the earlier title-reaching capture, keeping USB enabled, and sample the guest thread state at the freeze before changing behavior further.

## 2026-10-20 upstream APU fix cross-check
- Reviewed the current upstream XboxRecomp release and merged [PR #205](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/pull/205). Its measured Black-specific DirectSound stall was caused by the APU sample-limit interrupt and `XGSCNT` frequency: the title programmed SSL, reset the counter, enabled ISTS bit 3, and waited for its tick. Upstream changed the counter to 48 kHz and raises that interrupt. This is a credible lead, but the evidence is from Black and does not show Steel Battalion uses the same path.
- The local XboxRecomp v0.13.1 runtime still returns virtual-clock nanoseconds divided by 100 for `XGSCNT`, not a 48 kHz sample count. A bounded Steel Battalion audio-trace launch reached title-audio asset loading (through `SNK_009.wav`) but produced no `[APUMMIO]` trace entries and then exited on an access violation reading guest VA `0xFFFFFFF4` in the DSOUND code path. Because the trace failed to show register traffic and the crash was not reproduced, neither result confirms the SSL hypothesis or a general startup crash.
- No APU change was made. The targeted run did not verify audible output or reach the title prompt; no game process remains. The x64 MSVC Release toolchain is already installed and used for prior successful builds, so no Visual Studio installation was needed. Progress bars and Milestone 3 remain unchanged.
- Next verify why `RECOMP_APU_TRACE` emitted no register records, then determine from Steel Battalion's own APU writes whether it programs SSL/enables ISTS bit 3. Only if that path is observed should the narrowly scoped upstream fix be adapted and retested.

### 2026-10-20 — NV2A draw timing and upstream comparison

- Ran a bounded 90-second capture with per-draw timing and full pushbuffer method inventory. It ended at the watchdog after 164 flips; the visual state at stop was not captured, so this is a performance trace, not a boot verification or benchmark.
- Timing isolated the expensive path: 23 full-surface 640×480 textured vertex-program draws used `cpu-raster`, taking 1,146.7–1,274.7 ms each (median 1,224.1 ms). The path sampled DXT1/DXT5 and linear textures. The exact startup-logo Vulkan path took 7.8–45.0 ms (median 8.2 ms across 18 calls), and the opaque-black depth quad took 0.26–17.0 ms (median 0.29 ms across 16 calls). These are different draw cases; they show where time is spent, not a controlled same-draw speedup comparison.
- The leading unhandled-method counts were mostly clip bounds (`0x0394/0x0398`), wait-for-idle, lighting/specular state, and shader-stage input; they do not explain the measured raster time. Existing `PB-DRAW-STATE` logs show the slow path uses a full-surface quad, alpha blending, and an active texture/combiner state.
- Compared the pinned XboxRecomp v0.13.1 source with upstream v0.15.0 from GitHub. The newer pushbuffer executor has substantial NV2A draw-path changes; upstream's current path integrates with its D3D11 backend. Its MIT runtime and LGPL-2.1-or-later xemu-derived components are separately identified in `NOTICE`. No upstream code was copied and no runtime architecture/dependency change was made.

Both 640×480, 24-bit BMPs were byte-identical (zero differing bytes); the four transformed vertex positions, texture coordinates, and diffuse values also matched. The CPU logo draw took 1,224.5 ms; the same-state Vulkan draw took 56.1 ms on first use and about 8 ms on repeated draws. This verifies exact output and a draw-level speedup for these two startup states only, not other textures, later intro scenes, or general rendering. This is the leading liveness blocker; its root cause is still unknown.

### 2026-10-20 — title raster path narrowed

- Counted 65 timed draws in the title timing capture: 29 used `cpu-raster`, 19 the existing Vulkan logo path, 16 black-depth, and one black-fade. Thirty CPU-raster calls exceeded 100 ms; their mean was 1,133 ms and cumulative measured draw time was 34.0 seconds in the 70-second run. This is a measured boot-path blocker.
- Matched the slow 1024×1024 linear `0x12` draw to CPU-reference sampler behavior: filter `0x04074000` selects nearest sampling; address mode `3/3` clamps; depth testing is disabled; UVs are texel coordinates from 0..640 by 0..480. The current Vulkan helper uses linear/repeat sampling and normalized UVs, so routing this draw through it unchanged would be incorrect. A safe path needs nearest/clamp sampling, UV normalization, preserved alpha blending, and a same-draw pixel comparison. DXT3 and unknown format `0x2D` remain unresolved.

### 2026-10-20 — corrected DCF4 mapping and prompt-synchronized input pulse

Correction to the prior DCF4 note: `sub_0020EC60` does **not** wait on `0x0021DCF4`. With the observed context `0x0021B790`, it clears the signal at `context + 0x2558` and waits on event `context + 0x2554`, which is `0x0021DCE4`. The DCF4 waiter is in `sub_00211E10`: it clears `context + 0x2568` at guest instruction `0x00211E94` and waits on event `context + 0x2564` at `0x00211F39`, producing `0x0021DCF4`. Its setter path reaches `KeSetEvent` at caller `0x0021702F`.

A filtered, title-reaching trace observed DCF4 clears, waits, and sets on separate threads. One observed sequence was: waiter clears a preexisting signal, enters the wait with signal state 0, the D3D worker sets the event, then the waiter resumes, clears the signal, and waits again. This matches the guest's explicit clear-before-wait loop; it does not demonstrate a lost signal. A later watchdog sample was blocked on the distinct DCE4 event while the GPU queue drained. The DCF4 hypothesis is therefore narrowed and remains unproven as a cause of the title prompt/input behavior.

A separate prompt-synchronized test captured the title prompt before input, then sent a 500 ms Main Weapon (`a`) pulse at 25.9 seconds after the first controller report. The 26-byte Steel Battalion XID report changed byte 2 from `0x00` to `0x01` and back to `0x00`; OHCI served 1,085 periodic transfers and 1,074 reports. The post-input frame still showed `PRESS MAIN WEAPON BUTTON`. This verifies a prompt-time report reached the guest USB transfer path, not that the XInput handle or game state consumed it. Existing matched XInputGetState tracing found the guest's 24-byte device report still neutral during an earlier Main Weapon hold; trace the device handle/callback state alongside a confirmed prompt before adjusting mappings.

Public-source review: upstream [XboxRecomp v0.15.0](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp) and [PR #181](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/pull/181) add guest-event support for multiple-object waits, but do not establish a fix for this single-object DCF4 path. The [Steel Battalion Xemu fork](https://github.com/quizerno/Xemu-SB-VK) documents community input priorities but is an emulator fork, so no code was copied into this static recompilation project. Existing Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools and CMake were used; nothing new was installed.

Temporary DCF4 probes were restored byte-for-byte, the standard Windows x64 Release target rebuilt, and the rebuilt executable was checked for the temporary DCF4 marker strings. No game process remains. Captures and logs are ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-dcf4-actual-correlate-20261020/`, `m3-dcf4-stall-watchdog-20261020/`, `m3-title-main-weapon-test-20261020/`, and `m3-title-main-weapon-synced-20261020/`. ### 2026-10-20 — sustained Main Weapon input reaches USB; game state remains unresolved

A live 25-second Main Weapon hold while the title prompt was visible changed the virtual Steel Battalion report from `00 1A 00` to `00 1A 01`. The process reached its watchdog while the hold was still active; the screenshot remained at “PRESS MAIN WEAPON BUTTON.” At the 60-second watchdog, guest dwords `0x004AF63C` (first XInput payload word), `0x004B0118` (source of action byte `0x004AFBC3`), and `0x004AFBC0` (action-state dword) were all zero. The watchdog caught the guest at the wait on event `0x0021DCF4` (`sub_0020E5C0`); OHCI continued serving periodic reports. This shows a gap between host report delivery and title action state during this run, but it does not yet distinguish whether the XID device buffer was updated while held or whether the guest input poller was starved by its wait. A prior synchronized run did verify raw `01 00 00 00` in the XID handle and a later `XInputGetState` read, so the current result is a repeatability/timing discrepancy, not a replacement of that evidence.

The XboxRecomp guest write watcher on `0x004AFBC0` trapped the whole guest memory page and slowed the title substantially; discard that run for performance comparisons. No generated code was changed. Local ignored captures are under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-action-byte-write-watch-20261020/`, `m3-xinput-to-action-peek-20261020/`, `m3-action-hold-peek-20261020/`, and `m3-xid-handle-hold-peek-20261020/`. Thus at this sample it was waiting for completion count `0x13E`; the counter comparison explains the wait, but not why progress slowed. Concurrent NV2A USER PUT=`0x00066D10`, GET=`0x000647F0` shows pending work at that instant, not a proven root cause.

### 2026-10-20 — DCF4 signal verified; CPU raster fallback timed

- Repeated the 90-second real-DSP windowed run with the established diagnostic pair `RECOMP_TITLE_KEVENTS=1` and `RECOMP_VBLANK=1`, plus async I/O, pushbuffer execution, and the Steel Battalion USB profile. `RECOMP_AC97_READY=1` remained synthetic; DSP acknowledgement was unset. A DCF4-filtered wait trace confirmed a valid host event for guest object `0x0021DCF4`; `KeSetEvent` and the waiting guest thread alternated, typically about six seconds apart. The wait was not permanently lost. NV2A IRQs were claimed, and the queue advanced between samples. At the watchdog, USER PUT was `0x00064780`, GET `0x0005FD28`, with guest counters both `0xD0D`; this is a queue backlog at that instant, not proof of a specific stuck command.
- Timed draw tracing isolated a high-cost software fallback: 15 CPU-raster draws across formats `0x12`, `0x2D`, `0x0C`, and `0x0E` took 1.27–1.45 seconds each. The already gated Vulkan paths were far faster in the same diagnostic (the 1024×1024 linear-nearest path averaged 67 ms across four samples; 512×512 logo draws averaged 35 ms across 138 samples). `RECOMP_PB_TIMING` adds logging overhead, so these are diagnostic draw timings, not an FPS benchmark. The 90-second run did not verify a title screen.
- Repeated a 120-second run with `RECOMP_RASTER_THREADS=16` on the Ryzen 7 5700X3D (16 logical processors). In the later matching startup workload, software fallback still measured about 1.38–1.44 seconds per draw across the same common formats; this setting did not produce a useful improvement. The run reached 155 flips and ended at the watchdog. Do not claim a 60 FPS result or infer campaign performance from these splash measurements.
- Static state comparison explains why the existing fast path does not catch these CPU draws. The expensive `0x12` draw has blending disabled, depth test enabled with writes disabled, filter `0x02022000`, and combiner words `C4200000` / `D4301010`. The existing Vulkan nearest/clamp gate requires blending enabled, depth disabled, filter `0x04074000`, and combiner words `C4C80000` / `D4D81010`. These are different render states; routing them through the existing path without matching combiner/depth semantics and a same-draw pixel comparison would risk incorrect output.

Its D3D8 library documents register-combiner shader translation, while its NV2A sample remains register-level only; neither is a drop-in replacement for this title's raw pushbuffer workload and current Vulkan helper. No upstream code was copied. See the [XboxRecomp project documentation](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp) and the existing [Vulkan reuse audit](VULKAN_REUSE_AUDIT.md).
- The diagnostic-only source filter was reverted; the built Windows Release executable was rebuilt from the unmodified runtime source. The accidental edit to an unused duplicate `kernel_bridge.c` was restored byte-for-byte. No game process remains, no game data changed, and the user's XID patch remains untouched.
- Milestone 3 remains active; bars stay overall 2/8, boot/compatibility 3/5, virtual controller 2/3, DSP/audio 1/2. ### 2026-10-20 — upstream raster scheduling research

- Reviewed upstream XboxRecomp v0.15.0 and the merged [PR #228](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/pull/228), which queues many small triangles in a draw batch so worker threads do not sit idle below the per-triangle threshold. Its reported target case was thousands of small triangles per frame. Steel Battalion's measured slow draws are instead full-screen 640×480 triangles; the pinned runtime already splits each large triangle across worker rows, so this specific optimization is not supported by the current profile.
- Reviewed the still-open [PR #231](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/pull/231), which pipelines software-raster jobs behind pushbuffer decoding and snapshots per-pixel state. It addresses executor wait overhead for many batches, but requires drain points around state changes, clears, flips, reports, semaphores, and scan completion. The recent Steel Battalion capture sometimes had DMA PUT=GET while still slow, so current evidence does not establish that pipeline overlap would remove the dominant cost. This is a useful candidate, not a safe drop-in patch for the heavily modified v0.13.1 runtime.
- No upstream source was copied or renderer behavior changed. The current executable and source remain as previously verified; no new game run was performed. Upstream XBE tooling confirms the existing static recompilation choice remains appropriate; this blocker is in the title-specific GPU compatibility path, not function discovery or x86 translation.

### 2026-10-20 — title-reaching performance sample and failed repeat

A 180-second Windows x64 Release run reached the Steel Battalion title prompt (“PRESS MAIN WEAPON BUTTON”) at roughly 150–171 flips. The image was dim and the run's observed intervals were only about 0.4–1.8 FPS; it demonstrates title presentation once, not reliable boot. With `RECOMP_PB_TIMING=1`, 46 targeted 512x512 format-0x0E draws ran through the exact-state CPU combiner paths: passthrough (`D4301010`) median 14.27 ms (23 draws), and diffuse-times-texture (`D4D81010`) median 48.77 ms (23 draws). Earlier generic fallback measurements for these states exceeded 1.2 seconds per draw. The paths had separately passed 2,169,576 reference pixel comparisons with zero mismatches. The run's much higher whole-scene frame cost shows these paths are no longer the only bottleneck; 1024x1024 format-0x12 draws measured median 143.46 ms in the same capture. This is a bounded startup sample, not a stable performance benchmark.

A subsequent 240-second Main Weapon retry did not reach the title and did not send the input pulse. It remained in a repeated guest call path around `sub_0025C308` / `sub_0025BFA3`; the sampled framebuffer was black and output stopped advancing. The process was stopped after confirming no new guest progress. The previous intermittent access violation (`0xFE0001D8`) remains unexplained; one earlier title-reaching run did not reproduce it, while another retry crashed during DirectSound-style object cleanup. No root cause or fix has been established. Because this latest retry did not reach the prompt, it provides no evidence about the title action response.

The current XboxRecomp GitHub documentation describes an XBE conformance harness that compares lifted functions to original x86 code, but it only executes mechanically selected self-contained functions. That is not an appropriate direct probe for this failing runtime object/event path. The installed Ghidra XBE loader, XboxRecomp toolchain, Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools, and CMake are already available; no additional repository, SDK, or system dependency was installed. Architecture remains pinned XboxRecomp static translation plus the existing Vulkan renderer and compatibility runtime. No architecture change, game-derived file, or generated code is included in the repository.

### 2026-10-21 — APU front-end interrupt replay investigation

Compared the pinned v0.13.1 APU against the current official XboxRecomp source on GitHub. The upstream implementation documents a concrete interrupt bug: reasserting the front-end interrupt from every IRQ recomputation can replay a stale DirectSound voice event after the game's service routine acknowledges it, leading to an invalid object access. The local runtime had the same pattern in `update_irq`; the earlier guest crash occurred during the XBE's DirectSound release path with an address inside the synthetic kernel-thunk range. That connection is a strong hypothesis, not yet a proven root cause. Reference: [upstream APU implementation](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/src/apu/apu_core.c#L2481-L2540).

Added isolated patch `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-apu-front-end-interrupt-latch.patch`: latch `FETINTSTS` once at the emulated front-end trap event, and let `update_irq` recompute the line from pending status instead of recreating a cleared trap interrupt. Its reverse-apply check passes against the current patched runtime checkout. Removed the temporary peak instrumentation from ignored `apu_core.c`; the Windows x64 Release target builds successfully without it.

A 120-second APU-ready startup run with the Steel Battalion USB profile ended at the watchdog. It produced no access-violation record, but it also did not reach a title prompt and logged only 201 kernel calls before stopping at the existing `wbinvd` no-op report. This was not a reproduction of the more productive title-reaching trace setup, so it does not verify the interrupt fix or establish stable boot/audio. The process was stopped and no `your_game_recomp` process remains. Logs are ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-apu-interrupt-latch-retry-20261021/`.

Progress bars are unchanged: overall 2/8; XBE/decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; virtual controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2. Milestone 3 remains active. Next trace front-end trap/acknowledgment counts and identify the value that reaches the failing release call, then verify audio only after title audio starts. No rendering, controller, audio, or campaign feature is marked complete by this experiment.

### 2026-10-21 — APU physical-page ownership resolves the voice-table overlap

Followed the upstream XboxRecomp APU resolver’s page-ownership approach after reading its current `apu_core.c` explanation: physical addresses can overlap ordinary XBE RAM and the separately backed contiguous-memory window, so address-range/high-water checks alone cannot identify the intended backing page. The upstream project is MIT-licensed; the APU implementation it incorporates retains its existing xemu LGPL notices. Reference: https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/src/apu/apu_core.c.

A temporary allocation/voice trace proved Steel Battalion allocated contiguous memory at `0x80380000` for `0x8000` bytes, while voice 102’s table entry was at physical `0x00383300`, inside the XBE image address range. The old resolver selected ordinary image RAM. Added page ownership maps for allocated contiguous pages and pages explicitly named through `MmGetPhysicalAddress`; the APU now resolves named RAM first, then allocated contiguous pages, then the image and fallback ranges. The reusable change is isolated in `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-apu-physical-page-ownership.patch`; its reverse-apply check succeeds against the local pinned checkout.

Matched instrumented runs changed voice 102’s resolution from `0x00383300` to the contiguous alias `0x80383300`. Before the map fix, the voice list repeatedly remained at `0x0048` across 4,096 sampled idle events. With the map, the list contained `0x0066`, and only one idle event was sampled. This verifies the incorrect-page selection and corrected APU voice-table walk.

Removed all temporary trace code and rebuilt the Windows x64 Release target successfully. The clean 90-second launch produced the Steel Battalion “PRESS MAIN WEAPON BUTTON” prompt; its saved final frame is ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-apu-physical-page-ownership-verified-20261021/`. The watchdog ended the diagnostic with its expected code 3, after the run slowed from about 13.8 FPS to about 1 FPS. No crash was logged. Searched the rebuilt executable and runtime source for the temporary trace markers; none remain. No game process remains.

This run does not verify audible audio, game-accepted controller input, entering a mission, gameplay, saves, campaign progression, or a 60 FPS target. It also does not prove that the older DirectSound release crash was caused by the APU voice-table alias. Progress bars remain unchanged; Milestone 3 remains active. Next inspect the slow frame path and trace audio voice/mixbin output after the title prompt. No new Visual Studio or system-wide dependencies were needed; the existing Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools and project-local CMake built the target.

### 2026-10-21 — NV097 WAIT_FOR_IDLE modeled for the synchronous executor

### 2026-10-21 — full-screen CPU draw states compared with the exact Vulkan gate
- Inspected the retained timing records and the narrow `vulkan_linear_nearest_quad` gate in the pinned runtime. The recurring accelerated 1024×1024/format-0x12 draw has blending enabled (`SRC_ALPHA` / `ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA`) and depth disabled. CPU-raster draws of the same texture address/size are distinct guest states: some have blending disabled and depth enabled; others have blending enabled and depth enabled. The 512×512 DXT1 draws also have depth enabled. Matching only dimensions or texture address would therefore skip required blend/depth behavior.
- The trace supports keeping the acceleration gate exact. It identifies the common CPU states as a substantial-cost lead, but does not yet provide the guest state/combiner/vertex details needed to safely implement equivalent Vulkan paths. No renderer behavior changed and no new test was run.

### 2026-10-21 — upstream fence guidance narrows the sync lead
- Reviewed current XboxRecomp upstream source and its D3D8LTCG device-context notes. With the pushbuffer executor active, D3D needs honest semaphore progress, the fence wait must kick the work that releases its target, and the executor's GET position and PGRAPH `0x400B10` fence latch must agree. Upstream warns that an unconditional “GPU caught up” shortcut is unsafe with an active executor because it can let D3D overwrite queued commands and vertex data. References: [upstream D3D8LTCG device-context notes](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/docs/technical/d3d8ltcg-device-context.md) and [upstream pushbuffer executor](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/src/kernel/nv2a_pb_exec.c).
- The 95-second watchdog captured the title prompt while the guest stack was in `D3D_BlockOnTime` waiting on title event `0x0021DCF4`; the guest semaphore advanced from `0x445` to `0x569`. A separate trace recorded 32 waits and 31 matching `KeSetEvent` calls from another guest thread. This rules out treating one sampled wait or an invalid host handle as the cause. During the 180-second live-input run, XID report byte 2 reflected Main Weapon, but the guest title-query trace stopped at query 328; that run does not prove the game consumed the press.
- Next correlate the title's `device+0x30` semaphore pointer and fence target with PGRAPH `0x400B10`, executor GET, and `D3D_BlockOnTime` kickoff at the first stall. Do not add an unconditional caught-up shim or treat the upstream DMA-style walker as a drop-in fix. No runtime code changed; no build or replay was run for this research update.
- The research checkout is XboxRecomp v0.15.0; the project remains pinned to v0.13.1 with local uncommitted work. No upstream code was copied or merged. The user-edited `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-steel-battalion-xid-profile.patch` remains untouched and unstaged. Milestone 3 and all progress bars remain unchanged: overall 2/8; XBE/decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; virtual controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2.

### 2026-10-21 — static wait logic and upstream comparison
- Decompiled the exact Steel Battalion functions in Ghidra. `D3DDevice_Swap` calls `D3D_BlockOnTime`; when its `FUN_00211AB0` ring-distance helper stays at or above `0x2000` after kickoff, the code clears the event, rechecks the distance, then sleeps in `KeWaitForSingleObject` at `0x00211F3B`. The corrected watchdog's `EAX=0x24F8` and stack there match that branch. This identifies why the thread sleeps, but not yet why the helper remains high.
- The helper chooses a candidate GPU read pointer from the channel USER GET at `[device[0x8EF] + 0x44]`; if it falls outside the title's pushbuffer ring it falls back to the PFIFO `CACHE1_DMA_SUBROUTINE` value (`+0x324C`). It compares that read position with a pending fence node. The capture did not include the ring bounds or selected candidate, so we cannot yet tell which pointer source produced the `0x24F8` distance.
- Reviewed XboxRecomp's public v0.15.0 upstream device-context and pushbuffer-executor docs/source alongside the pinned local v0.13.1 runtime. Upstream documents a similar `Swap` wait with USER PUT=GET, and warns that advancing GET early or faking a completed fence can let D3D overwrite commands still needed by the executor. Its newer DMA walk and fence-model changes are a lead to compare, not a drop-in patch: Steel Battalion's actual stream and local runtime revision must be verified first. References: [device-context notes](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/docs/technical/d3d8ltcg-device-context.md) and [pushbuffer executor notes](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/docs/technical/pushbuffer-executor.md). No upstream code was copied or merged.
- Existing local Ghidra-XBE tooling, Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools, and CMake handled the analysis/build; no additional tools were installed. Next collect the device ring bounds, USER GET, PFIFO `CACHE1_DMA_SUBROUTINE`, candidate selected by the helper, and fence-node address/value at the first threshold crossing. Keep the real semaphore and do not apply an always-caught-up shim. Milestone 3/bars remain unchanged; input acceptance, audio, mission, save/load, and campaign completion remain unverified.

Ghidra maps return address `0x0020E5C0` to the call after `D3DDevice_Swap` invokes `D3D_BlockOnTime`; the call argument was fence value `0x609`, flags `1`. The helper result in EAX was `0x44B8`, above the XBE's `0x2000` wait threshold. NV2A USER PUT/GET were `0x0006A818` / `0x000629B8`, so this sample had queued work. This is strong evidence that input reaches the game while presentation is stuck in its D3D fence path; it does not establish why the work or fence fails to complete.

### 2026-10-21 — fence helper distance trace
- Used the already-installed Ghidra-XBE analysis to follow the exact XBE routine `sub_00211AB0`, which `D3D_BlockOnTime` calls before each wait. The helper selects the USER DMA GET pointer when it lies in the ring (otherwise PFIFO's subroutine GET), normalizes it against the ring base, subtracts that cursor from the fence descriptor's target offset, and returns zero if the target is already behind the cursor. This explains what the reported wait distance represents; it does not explain why the distance remains high.
- A temporary diagnostic at that helper recorded repeated nonzero distances `0x24F8`, `0x4A08/0x4A20`, and `0x6F30`, above the guest's `0x2000` wait threshold. Samples showed USER GET advancing through `0xFA08`, `0x14440`, `0x18E60`, `0x1D898`, `0x238E0`, and `0x2B728`; the D3D sequence/semaphore values also advanced. The prompt remained visible and title-query tracing continued, but the appended input in this run was not confirmed by an XID report or nonzero title query. Therefore this is a queue/fence diagnostic only, not a second input-acceptance result or proof of a deadlock.
- No `[PB-FENCE]` event was captured in this run. The diagnostic printed a stack word under a misleading `caller` label; do not treat that field as a caller address. Restored generated C byte-for-byte (SHA-256 `2C9B70812633A82ABFC8C2EA2CC2D6B3FA400818385AF341EC73B93ED9BB7867`). The first MSBuild invocation reused the old object and left the temporary marker in the executable; after touching the restored source, the Release x64 target recompiled `recomp_0015.c` and linked successfully. Verified `[FENCE-HELPER]` and `[SB-DF770]` are absent from the rebuilt executable. The temporary diagnostic process was stopped; no game process remains. No runtime fix or upstream code was incorporated.
- Milestone 3 and all progress bars remain unchanged. Next capture the helper's selected pointer source, device base/ring offset, fence target, and semaphore in the same sample; then compare the wait against actual executor progress at the corresponding frame. Do not synthesize fence completion. Stable title transition, audio, mission loading, save/load, and campaign completion remain unverified.

### 2026-10-21 — executor timing explains the recurring Swap wait
- Cross-checked the helper trace against the pinned runtime's execution order. `nv2a_ack_thread` calls `nv2a_pb_scan`; that walker calls `nv2a_pb_exec_packet` synchronously, and the thread writes USER DMA GET to PUT only after the submitted range has been scanned and executed. The D3D helper's positive target-minus-GET distance is therefore real back-pressure while the runtime is consuming the commands.
- Correction: the 43.7–53.9 ms CPU-raster draws and 484–968 ms submitted ranges came from a headless capture where the Vulkan framebuffer presenter was absent. They describe the CPU fallback and cannot explain the windowed title run. The newer windowed fence-helper capture selected the RTX 4070 SUPER and logged `[Vulkan] textured logo fade completed at 640x480`; it still reached only the title prompt and did not confirm the post-input screen.
- The recurring wait is confirmed queue back-pressure, but its interactive service cost and exact blocking draw remain unmeasured. Do not bypass the guest wait or report GET early: that lets the guest overwrite unread commands and has produced unstable/lost draws. Next collect low-volume `RECOMP_PB_TIMING` with the Vulkan window enabled, then pair any slow draw with the fence-helper target/GET and presentation count. Milestone 3 and all bars remain unchanged; audio, stable post-title transition, missions, save/load, and campaign completion remain unverified.

### 2026-10-21 — community Vulkan renderer suitability check
- Found a relevant original-Xbox static-recompilation project: [nfsu2-sw](https://github.com/antoxa2584x/nfsu2-sw) has a Vulkan 1.3 backend for an NV2A pushbuffer executor. Its documented design includes guest surfaces, texture/pipeline caches, and GPU draws, so it is a useful architecture reference. Its Vulkan notes report race performance roughly equal to its GL build on Switch; they do not establish results on Windows or this title. Our local tree already has a Vulkan window and an exact-state textured-logo path, so the immediate task is measuring that implementation's windowed behavior.

The public renderer targets a newer executor backend-registration interface (`nv2a_backend.h`), which is absent from our pinned local XboxRecomp v0.13.1 tree. The public renderer source also has no license header, and the repository's `LICENSE` endpoint returns 404 while its README makes no project-license grant; therefore no code was copied. Its source includes pthread, SDL Vulkan, and glslang dependencies, and the project documents Linux/Switch builds rather than Windows/MSVC, so Windows compatibility is unverified. References: [renderer source](https://github.com/antoxa2584x/nfsu2-sw/blob/main/xboxrecomp/src/nv2a_vk/nv2a_vk.c), [project README](https://github.com/antoxa2584x/nfsu2-sw/blob/main/README.md), [Vulkan notes](https://github.com/antoxa2584x/nfsu2-sw/blob/main/VULKAN_NOTES.md).

The public renderer source has no license header, and the repository's `LICENSE` endpoint returns 404 while its README makes no project-license grant, so no code was copied. Its source includes pthread, SDL Vulkan, and glslang dependencies, and its documented builds cover Linux/Switch rather than Windows/MSVC. No code or dependency changed in this review. Milestone 3 and bars remain unchanged; continue profiling the existing windowed path and use public work only as a reference unless its license and compatibility are established.

## 2026-10-21 — windowed timing isolated a slow F16 draw
- A 120-second bounded windowed run used the clean x64 executable with the Vulkan framebuffer window on the RTX 4070 SUPER and `RECOMP_PB_TIMING=1`. The presenter initialized at 640×480 and completed the textured logo fade. The watchdog ended the run; no game process remains. The run did not schedule input or enable the title-state trace, so it is a performance capture only.
- Windowed per-draw timing recorded a repeated CPU-raster quad sampling guest offset `0x800021E9`, reported as 1607×3, `D3DFMT_F16` (`0x2D`), pitch 64. Individual draws took about 735–749 ms. Pushbuffer segments took about 3.4–3.5 seconds; the final GPU summary counted 1,180 draws and still reported 0.00 FPS at 186 flips. This is a measured renderer bottleneck in the active windowed path and a plausible contributor to queue lag; this run did not pair the draw with the precise guest fence wait, so causation is not proved.
- The reported texture width and pitch are inconsistent with an ordinary 16-bit linear texture (`1607 × 2` bytes per row exceeds pitch 64). Do not add a generic F16 sampler or fast path based only on this descriptor. Xemu's NV2A implementation defines the F16 depth conversion; the separate Xbox XBE recomp reference maps F16 depth to a host R32F representation. These are research references only; no upstream code was copied. Next establish how this descriptor is produced and what guest memory it actually addresses, then use a narrowly state-gated optimization only if it can be compared against the existing software framebuffer.
- The 120-second watchdog did not prove the title transition, input, audio, or a mission. No game/runtime source changed; no build was run in this diagnostic. Ignored artifacts are under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-windowed-pb-timing-20261021/`. Milestone 3 and all progress bars remain unchanged. Audio, stable post-title rendering, campaign, saves, and mission compatibility remain unverified.

## 2026-10-21 — timed draw-state capture found a repeated L8 full-screen hot path
- Supersedes the assumption that the slow path is specific to the earlier F16 sample. A 90-second windowed run combined `RECOMP_PB_TIMING` with the one-frame trace. The traced frame itself contained a 1024×1024 no-depth texture quad; later per-draw timing/state records captured the recurring slow batch.
- The repeated draw sampled guest offset `0x8000008A`, 53×1, swizzled L8 (`fmt 0x00`), pitch 64. It covered the full 640×480 surface, used register-combiner stage program `0x3`, bilinear/wrap filtering, blend `SRC_ALPHA/INV_SRC_ALPHA`, and LEQUAL depth test. The four corners had differing UV and diffuse values. Each CPU-raster draw took 747–760 ms; the surrounding submitted segment took about 1.7 seconds. The same draw/state pair recurred throughout the final trace. The guest watchdog still found a Swap fence wait (`EAX=0x4A28`); USER DMA PUT/GET were `0x7A424`/`0x72B30`.
- The local sampler maps swizzled L8 (`0x00`) to its linear twin `LIN_L8` (`0x13`) and has a decoder for it, so this is not a missing L8-format case. The cost is in the general full-screen CPU-raster path for this blend/depth/combiner state. The earlier F16 measurement remains a valid separate sample, but is not the sole or established cause of the recurring slow draws. Queue lag and CPU draw time correlate in this run; the exact fence dependency is still unproved.
- No game/runtime source changed and no build was run. One frame trace was collected, but it did not include the later 53×1 draw. Watchdog ended PID 64592; no process remains. Artifacts are ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-f16-correlated-timing-20261021/`. Next compare a narrowly gated Vulkan quad path that preserves this exact texture sampling, vertex interpolation, blend and depth behavior against the software framebuffer; do not skip depth or guest synchronization. ### 2026-10-21 — page watch identifies the guest write boundary
- A corrected-async 110-second replay captured the first XPR sentinel transition at `0x810D900C`. A five-word snapshot around the transition showed the first word change from `0x000800A5` to `0x00FFFFFD`; at the same sampling point, words at `+4`, `+0xC`, and `+0x10` also read `0x00FFFFFD`, while `+8` continued its regular counter increments. The watched write fault resolved to guest `sub_0020EF70+0x14F`, generated `recomp_0015.c:27049`, the `MEM32(eax) = edx` store in the old-texture reference-count decrement path.
- This identifies a guest store boundary, not the original source of the invalid value. The page-watch temporarily makes the whole page writable, so another guest thread can write during that window without a separately observed fault. Do not conclude that one SetTexture store changed all neighboring words, or that SetTexture caused the initial corruption. The observed store is consistent with consuming a resource word that is already in a bad state; its actual pre-store value and originating write remain unknown.
- No matching `NtReadFile` overlap or contiguous free was observed in earlier captures. No runtime fix or architecture change was made. Removed the temporary five-word watcher extension and rebuilt x64 Release successfully; confirmed its marker is absent and no game process remains. The trace ended at the 110-second watchdog and did not verify title progression, audio, or gameplay.
- Next instrument the guest SetTexture decrement path at the actual instruction boundary to log its stage, resource pointer, pre/post resource word, and nearby fields, then trace the first write that introduces the inconsistent value. Avoid changing resource lifetime semantics until that source is confirmed. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged.

### 2026-10-21 — APU DSP DMA confirmed as an XPR overwrite path
- Used an exact x64 hardware data breakpoint and an opt-in DSP transfer trace, then correlated the writes with the GP DMA node. The guest node performs a 16-word, 64-byte DSP-to-guest transfer through scratch buffer E (`base=0x044E20`, `offset=0x013A00`, `size=0x017700`, effective scratch `0x00058820`). Its SGE table (`GPSADDR=0x003C4000`) entry 88 contains physical address `0x010D87C0`. Adding the page offset `0x820` resolves the DMA destination to `0x810D8FE0`; the 64-byte write crosses into XPR object `0x810D9000` and covers refcount dword `0x810D900C`. Captured payload words included `0x00FFEB98`, then `0x000000A5` on the next observed transfer.
- This establishes that the reconstructed DSP DMA path actually overwrites the XPR object through the guest-programmed SGE mapping. It does **not** establish whether the guest SGE entry is invalid, the ring-buffer mapping is being interpreted incorrectly, or the guest's DSP program intentionally reuses that memory. The SGE entry is unaligned (`0x010D87C0`); nearby contiguous allocation tracing shows XPR begins at `0x010D9000`, but does not reveal who wrote SGE entry 88 or what its intended owner is. No behavioral fix was applied.
- External project watchdog stopped the instrumented capture after 90 seconds; it had advanced to roughly 493 flips, but this run is not evidence of mission boot, valid audio output, or campaign compatibility. The first and second DMA captures reproduced the 64-byte overlap. Temporary traces were removed; x64 Release rebuilt successfully. Confirmed temporary trace strings are absent from the executable and no game process remains. Capture logs are ignored under `iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-xpr-dma-node-capture-20261021/`.
- Reviewed the upstream xemu MCPX APU/DSP implementation as a behavioral reference; no code was copied. Visual Studio 2022 Build Tools were already installed and used for both builds.
- **Next:** trace guest writes that populate `GPSADDR` and SGE[88], identify the scratch ring allocation and owner, and compare the DSP node program with xemu's DMA node semantics. Keep DMA behavior and XPR/refcount semantics unchanged until the mapping's intended ownership is established. Milestone 3 and progress bars remain unchanged: overall 2/8; decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2.

### 2026-10-21 — root cause found and page-aware DSP DMA fix verified
- Watched guest SGE[88] during startup. Translated game audio code (`sub_00228B3A`, the SGE builder) wrote `0x010D87C0` after its physical-address callback (`0xFE000190`) received the same input `0x010D87C0`; the owning guest data object began at `0x0108C7C0`. This confirms the SGE entry came from the game's audio buffer mapping.
- The real collision was in the host DSP physical resolver. At the DMA event, page `0x010D8` was marked both game-named physical RAM and allocated contiguous memory (`phys_named=1`, `contig_page=1`), while contiguous high-water had reached `0x0113A000`. A title-specific DSP helper chose the contiguous alias from the high-water value and bypassed `mcpx_apu_phys`, even though that resolver already gives named physical RAM precedence. This redirected the DSP write to the separate `0x810D...` alias containing the XPR object.
- Removed the high-water shortcut and delegated DSP physical resolution to `mcpx_apu_phys`. In the matched verification capture, the same DSP node resolved to low-RAM host address `0x010E8FE0`; the low-RAM target stayed zero and the XPR alias word `0x810D900C` stayed `0x00040001` before and after each of two writes. The captured incoming DSP word was zero in that run; earlier captures of the same transfer carried `0x00FFEB98` and `0x000000A5`. The mapping fix, rather than payload content, kept those writes out of the XPR alias.
- Added `patches/xboxrecomp-v0.13.1-dsp-sge-physical-alias-correction.patch`, which removes the unconditional alias shortcut after the earlier experiment patch. Verified that it applies cleanly after that patch. The prior `dsp-sge-physical-alias.patch` is superseded; use the correction immediately after it in a patch stack.
- The fix-enabled run presented the startup logo and reached about 591 flips before the external 75-second bound; it did not verify audio output, mission entry, or campaign behavior. Temporary kernel/APU/generated-code probes were removed. Generated `recomp_0016.c` matches baseline SHA-256 `5F12E72D5156F5353BE2944485C76B5665904CDD9528936ECF11F9D3579134AA`. The clean x64 Release build succeeded at `local-tools/steel-battalion-game/build-xpp-seeded/Release-verification/your_game_recomp.exe`; the normal Release output stayed locked by Windows during link. A scan found no temporary probe strings in the clean executable, and no game process remains. Logs are local/ignored under the `m3-xpr-sge88-*` and `m3-xpr-sge-physical-alias-*` directories.
- Progress bars remain unchanged: overall 2/8; decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2. **Next:** replay the known title/menu input sequence with the corrected resolver, confirm the XPR value remains valid through menu progression, then check whether the DSP output becomes audible. Keep Milestone 3 active.

### 2026-10-21 — four raster workers improve startup throughput
- A 30-second A/B profile ran the same 512×512 format-0xC CPU-raster path with one versus four total workers. This is a bounded startup workload, not an FPS or campaign benchmark.
