### 2026-10-21 — Ghidra pinpoints the DirectSound null-list state
- Re-read the captured exception against Ghidra's function body and the generated XBE call site. `CDirectSound_CommitDeferredSettings` at `0x0021FE7C` walks a circular LIST_ENTRY whose sentinel is the device object at `this+0x10`. At the crash, the guest registers show EDI=`0x01081004`, EBX=`0`, and ESI=`0xFFFFFFEC`; the sentinel address is therefore `0x01081014`, but its forward link read as null. The loop's `current-20` calculation turned that null entry into ESI=`0xFFFFFFEC`; `CDirectSoundVoice_CommitDeferredSettings` then faulted reading VA `0xFFFFFFF4`. This establishes the immediate failure precisely: the list forward link was null where a circular-list node or the sentinel was required.
- The list should be empty by linking the sentinel back to itself, so this capture is inconsistent with a correctly initialized empty list. It does not yet say whether initialization was skipped, the DirectSound object was released/reused, or another path overwrote the link. The concurrent critical-section waiter is evidence of contention, not proof that the critical section caused the bad pointer.
- GitHub research found Cxbx-Reloaded's Xbox Online Dashboard symbol database labels DirectSound `AllocateVoices`, `FreeVoices`, `Commit3dSettings`, and the DirectSound critical-section global. This is a cross-title symbol lead only; it is not Steel Battalion code and was not copied. Compare these routines/fields against the XBE's own code before using any behavior assumptions. Reference: [Cxbx-Reloaded Xbox Online Dashboard symbols](https://github.com/Cxbx-Reloaded/sym2idc/blob/master/FFFE0000_4C0590E3_Xbox%20OnlineDash.sym).
- No source or runtime changes this turn; no game process remains and the ISO is unchanged. Progress bars remain unchanged: overall 2/8; decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2. Next locate initialization and mutation of the `this+0x10` list field in Steel Battalion's own XBE, then capture the value transition to zero. ### Upstream check — avoid the unfinished DMA pushbuffer walk
- Reviewed XboxRecomp's upstream v0.16.0 release notes and current `nv2a_pb_exec.c` while investigating the title's D3D wait. The release notes state that `RECOMP_PB_WALK=dma` is opt-in and that its D3D fence wait sleeps on a software-method interrupt the runtime does not yet raise. The current Steel Battalion diagnostics enable the existing `RECOMP_PB_EXEC=1` executor and do not set `RECOMP_PB_WALK=dma`; this upstream warning therefore does not explain the observed 381-flip stall by itself.
- Keep the established executor setting for further traces. Do not turn on the DMA walk or transplant the held upstream draft as a speculative fix. A later, focused comparison should map the current `0x0021DCF4` wait to the actual pushbuffer/fence state before considering a narrow upstream backport. No dependency or source code was changed.
- Reference: [XboxRecomp releases](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/releases) (v0.16.0 pushbuffer-executor notes).

### Focused trace — D3D fence backlog and intermittent DirectSound list fault
- Repeated the title startup with the existing XBE event/vblank and pushbuffer settings, a 130-second watchdog snapshot, guest memory peeks, and a write watch on DirectSound device list head `0x01081014`. The renderer reached 1,146 flips and 24,457 draw submissions; the recorded late samples were about 7–9 FPS. Title input queries continued and returned neutral because this run sent no input.
- At the watchdog, the guest stack was in `D3DDevice_Swap`/`D3D_BlockOnTime` waiting on event `0x0021DCF4` for fence `0x155B`. The completion semaphore at `0x80003000` was `0x1557`, four values behind, and NV2A USER PUT/GET were `0x23878`/`0x1E420`, with queued bytes remaining. The event signal state was zero at this snapshot. This identifies unfinished pushbuffer/fence work as the immediate wait condition in this sample; it does not prove a permanent deadlock.
- The list-head watch captured initialization to self-link and insertion of voice node `0x803DCD78`, then no later write through the 130-second capture. A separate un-watched run crashed in the game's audio update (`FUN_0010D6D0` caller at `0x0010DB19`) when `IDirectSound_CommitDeferredSettings` (`0x0021FE7C`) iterated the same device head at `0x01081014` after it was zeroed. The null-list crash therefore reproduced, but its zeroing writer was not captured and the watched run did not reproduce it. Keep fence delay and list corruption as separate issues.
- The diagnostic process ended at its watchdog snapshot; no game process remains and the original ISO was untouched. No runtime source changed. Logs remain ignored under `local-tools/steel-battalion-game/iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-title-fence-peek-20261021/` and `m3-dsound-null-writer-20261021/`.
- Progress bars remain unchanged: overall 2/8; decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2. Milestone 3 remains active. Next reproduce the DirectSound failure with the list watch and capture the nulling write, while separately using state-driven input during a run whose fence samples continue advancing.

### 2026-10-21 — DirectSound crash setup isolated; correct configuration still stalls
- Used the existing XboxDev Ghidra XBE analysis and XboxRecomp v0.16.0 review while narrowing the intermittent DirectSound fault. Built the local x64 executable with crash-only guest-memory snapshots; the snapshots are local diagnostics and did not change guest behavior. Visual Studio Build Tools build completed successfully.
- A replay with `RECOMP_TITLE_KEVENTS=1`, `RECOMP_VBLANK=1`, `RECOMP_PB_EXEC=1`, and USB enabled, but without `RECOMP_AC97_READY=1`, reproduced a null-this fault in the XBE's DirectSound deferred-settings walk. At the fault, `0x006C02B8` pointed to game sound object `0x006C1E80`, whose first field was zero; the DirectSound voice-list sentinel `0x01081014` was correctly self-linked. Ghidra call flow shows startup publishes the game sound object before DirectSound creation completes, and its failure path returns early; the later audio callback checks only the outer object and passes the null interface pointer. The local runtime documents that omitting `RECOMP_AC97_READY` makes the emulated AC'97 codec report absent and causes DirectSoundCreate to fail. This crash was therefore a misconfigured diagnostic replay, not evidence that the DirectSound voice list is corrupt.
- Repeated with `RECOMP_AC97_READY=1` and the APU, title-event, vblank, pushbuffer, and USB settings enabled. The null-this crash did not recur during the 110-second bounded capture, but the renderer stayed at one flip, XAudio2 submitted only zero PCM, and title input tracing never became active. This does not verify a title boot or audible audio.
- The earlier separate crash in `sub_0021ED29` with a non-null device pointer and invalid guest address remains unresolved. Do not alter DirectSound list or synchronization semantics. Logs are ignored under `local-tools/steel-battalion-game/iso/local-analysis/logs/m3-dsound-list-crash-snapshot-complete-20261021/` and `m3-dsound-ac97-enabled-20261021/`; no game process remains and the ISO was untouched. The user's XID patch remains unstaged.
- Progress remains overall 2/8; decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2. Milestone 3 stays active. Next repeat the known-good title path with AC'97 readiness enabled and capture the separate non-null-pointer DirectSound fault while correlating XBE callers and the active voice node. No architecture change is warranted.

### 2026-10-22 — D3D fence wait narrowed to completion lag and event wake
- With AC'97 readiness, DSP acknowledgement, async I/O, title events, vblank, pushbuffer execution, and the Steel Battalion USB profile enabled, the XBE watchdog reproduced a wait at `D3DDevice_Swap` / `D3D_BlockOnTime`, guest event `0x0021DCF4`. A diagnostic-only watchdog snapshot now records the game completion semaphore, D3D fence counters, and PGRAPH pattern register in the ignored local XboxRecomp checkout.
- One 38-second sample had USER PUT/GET `0x000556EC` / `0x0004E7A4`, semaphore `0x4CD`, and submitted fence `0x4D9` (12 apart). A separate sample drained USER PUT/GET to `0x000049D0` but still showed semaphore `0x6C1` versus fence `0x6C5` (four apart), with the guest waiting on `0x0021DCF4`. These are bounded snapshots and show incomplete completion at the sample time; they do not establish a permanent deadlock or identify why the final four releases are delayed.
- A packet/IRQ trace confirmed the title connected NV2A vector 3 to XBE handler `0x002169F0`. It observed a nonzero `NV097_NO_OPERATION` packet (`0x01080021`) and a claimed NV2A interrupt. Public xqemu source documents this nonzero NOP as a synchronous PGRAPH notification that waits for guest acknowledgement; the local executor has the corresponding notify path. This supports the existing interrupt model but does not verify that this notification signals Steel Battalion's separate `0x0021DCF4` wait event. The follow-up run did not reach that title wait, and its event trace was capped before it could establish a DCF4 setter.
- The x64 Release target rebuilt successfully after the opt-in watchdog snapshot was added. The first build exposed a C pointer-arithmetic error in the diagnostic itself; it was corrected before the successful build. No game or renderer semantics changed. Three bounded replays were variable; the latest ended at a 36-second watchdog in early startup with USER PUT=GET=`0x495C`, so it is not comparable to the title-wait captures. No game process remains, the original ISO is untouched, and the user's XID patch is still unstaged.
- References reviewed: [xqemu NV2A PGRAPH source](https://github.com/xqemu/xqemu/blob/master/hw/xbox/nv2a/nv2a_pgraph.c) and [XboxRecomp upstream source](https://github.com/sp00nznet/xboxrecomp/blob/main/src/kernel/nv2a_pb_exec.c). No code was copied or dependency installed.
- Progress remains overall 2/8; decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2. Milestone 3 remains active. Next capture `KeSetEvent` / guest SignalState specifically for `0x0021DCF4` in a title-reaching run, alongside semaphore and USER PUT/GET; then verify whether the wake permits new title input polling. Audible output, full original controller behavior, mission gameplay, save/load, and campaign completion remain unverified.

### Correction — DCF4 event signaling is active; completion backlog persists
- Added targeted logging that bypasses the earlier 64-event trace cap only for guest event `0x0021DCF4`, plus watchdog capture of its header and SignalState. The x64 Release target rebuilt successfully; changes remain diagnostic-only in ignored `local-tools` source.
- In a 70-second bounded startup, the log captured 415 `KeSetEvent` calls and 416 waits for DCF4 across about 49 seconds. Each sampled wait began unsignaled, followed by a set; later waits observed the guest's reset. The DCF4 event is therefore being signaled and consumed repeatedly in this run. This corrects the previous “setter unverified” status and makes a missing DCF4 setter an unlikely explanation for the recurring wait.
- At the watchdog, guest EIP again waited at `0x0020E5C0` on `0x0021DCF4`; guest SignalState was 0 because the event was in its wait/reset cycle. USER PUT/GET were `0x00048784` / `0x00043D64`, the semaphore was `0x00000D01`, and the submitted fence was `0x00000D0D`. This sample had queued NV2A work and a 12-count completion gap. It establishes persistent work backlog at that instant, but not whether the gap was growing, stable, or caused by translation/raster cost.
- The diagnostic game exited at its 70-second watchdog (code 3); no game process remains and the ISO is untouched. The original XID patch remains unstaged. Next collect low-overhead periodic USER PUT/GET, semaphore, fence, and executor-time samples in one title-reaching run to distinguish a growing queue from a bounded pipeline delay. Do not alter event or completion semantics based on the current snapshots.
- Progress remains overall 2/8; decompilation 2/4; boot/compatibility 4/5; startup rendering 2/4; controller 2/3; DSP/audio 1/2. Milestone 3 remains active. Stable boot, audible audio, full original-controller behavior, gameplay, save/load, and campaign completion remain unverified.

### Upstream XboxRecomp investigation — DMA GET visibility
- Reviewed upstream XboxRecomp v0.16.0 (`f0f8a46`, released 2026-10-20) and its pushbuffer-walk change. It adds an opt-in DMA-accurate walk that publishes `DMA_GET` as commands are fetched. The upstream notes explain that XDK D3D reads GET during fence waits and may insert a software notification method when the GPU is behind; a GET value that only advances after a complete scan can therefore make it patch a command the executor already passed.
- This matches the shape of our delayed GET updates and outstanding-fence snapshots more closely than the earlier missing-event hypothesis. Our current runtime does handle nonzero `NV097_NO_OPERATION` through a synchronous PGRAPH notification, and DCF4 sets are verified, but delivery of the exact inserted notification during a progressing DMA walk is not yet proven.
- Upstream deliberately makes the DMA walk opt-in until a title's PGRAPH software-method interrupt path is confirmed. Do not replace the current scanner or make this behavior the default. Next add a bounded, opt-in comparison using the existing guest-safe runtime and capture GET, fence completion, DCF4, and title progress; keep a saved copy of the ignored local runtime source and retain the existing mode as the control.
- Reviewed [XboxDev/ghidra-xbe](https://github.com/XboxDev/ghidra-xbe) as an XBE loader and symbol-recovery aid. It is useful for further static analysis but is not needed to explain this runtime synchronization lead. No upstream source was copied, no dependency was installed, and the VS 2022 Build Tools already present are sufficient for the current target.
- Milestone 3 remains active; progress stays overall 2/8, decompilation 2/4, boot/compatibility 4/5, startup rendering 2/4, controller 2/3, and DSP/audio 1/2. Stable boot, audible output, full original-controller behavior, gameplay, save/load, and campaign completion remain unverified.
